### 318. assistant (2026-06-19T08:18:23.860Z)

Status checkpoint — substantial progress, two background jobs cooking, and three infra/bug issues found (one already merged).

## ✅ Sync + sanity (Phase 0–1) — done, and it caught a real bug
- Synced the working tree to `origin/master` (your #220 + the refactor PRs). Local `master` was 50 commits behind and locked by a stale worktree, so I based on `origin/master` directly.
- **Found a master-breaking bug and fixed it → [#237](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/237) (merged):** `tests/test_ablation.py` called `ablation._is_fusion_schema`, which the #215/#235 SSOT refactor moved to `experiment`. It throws **at collection time** → `pytest` exit 2 → the **whole local suite was interrupted**. CI stayed green only because the golden glob is empty there (same green-CI/red-locally shape as the served-gate litmus). After the fix: **904 passed, coverage 82.98%**, ruff+mypy clean.
- **Quality not regressed:** n=15 eval identical to baseline (tIoU F1@0.5 **0.468**, R2 tolF1 **0.353**). The nightly regression's only complaint is benign — the frozen baseline is n=13, corpus is now n=15 (a re-baseline maintenance item, queued).

## 🔄 Local >7s extraction (Phase 2) — running (~2.5hr ETA)
- All **5 proxies built** (GPU NVDEC→NVENC, ~4.5× realtime; the 4K is **HEVC 10-bit** which `h264_nvenc` can't encode → I force 8-bit). WASB GPU detection now running on GX010142 → GX010143/144/145/147.
- Reels publish to a **new dir on G:**: `…/Boxhill/2026-06-19 Boxhill - Local no-AI (7s+ rallies)`. (First launch hit a self-inflicted bug — my dir name had a literal `>` which is illegal on Windows; fixed, proxies were reused so ~no time lost.)
- Config = your shipped **cap=6 + R4 milestone** (verified effective), `--min-rally-duration 7`, fully local (no Gemini).
- **Heads-up / your P7 finding in the wild:** cap=6 splits long windows at gaps, so >7s yield is clip-dependent — on the closest analog (GX010137) cap=6 yields only ~3 >7s rallies. Some 19-June reels may be short. That under-production of long rallies is itself a **gap vs Gemini** I'll quantify.

## 🔄 Gemini comparison (Phase 3) — running, after I fixed a second infra issue
- **Infra finding (important for production):** the config's default model alias **`gemini-flash-latest` is currently degraded** — **11.6s for a *trivial text* query** → video chunks blow past the 180s timeout → the path silently returns **0 rallies** (this is why my earlier Gemini run "hung"). **`gemini-2.5-flash` responds in 1.3s** (healthy). I pinned 2.5-flash for this reference run (now yielding 8–10 segments/chunk). I did **not** change the repo default model — that's your product call — but you should know the shipped alias is unreliable right now.

## New PRs
- [#238](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/238): `--min-rally-duration` CLI flag on the runner (surfaces #220's knob; used for this extraction).

## What's next (auto-continues when the jobs finish — they notify me)
1. **Learnings: local-vs-Gemini gaps** on the 3 clips (counts, durations, >7s yield, where each over/under-fires) — the comparison you want, for your golden-labeling pass.
2. **Deferred due-diligence PRs** (after extraction frees the checkout): nightly re-baseline (n=15), and a robustness fix so a degraded model fails **loudly** instead of silently returning 0 rallies.

I'll keep going autonomously and report the learnings once the reels + Gemini detections land.

### 337. assistant (2026-06-19T08:54:30.701Z)

"""Local vs Gemini gap analysis on the Boxhill 19-June clips (owner learnings request).

For each clip with both signals: local rallies (output/19june.db, the shipped cap=6 milestone
windowing) vs Gemini rallies (output/gemini_<stem>_19june.json, reference). Reports total/long
counts, duration distributions, and IoU agreement (which rallies both find, where each over/under-
fires) — the gap picture for the owner's golden-labeling pass. Also re-windows the cached WASB
trajectory uncapped to show the cap=6 effect on >7s yield. CPU-only; gitignored scratch."""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import sqlite3
import statistics
import sys

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))

from backend.eval import metrics
from backend.utils.hashing import compute_video_id

PROXY_DIR = r"F:/KhelSutra_work/19june_proxies"
STEMS = ["GX010142", "GX010143", "GX010145"]   # clips with both local + Gemini
LOCAL_DB = os.path.join("output", "19june.db")
LONG = 7.0


def _local_rallies(stem: str):
    proxy = os.path.join(PROXY_DIR, f"{stem}_1080p.mp4")
    if not os.path.exists(proxy):
        return None
    vid = compute_video_id(proxy)
    con = sqlite3.connect(LOCAL_DB)
    rows = con.execute(
        "SELECT start_time, end_time FROM play_segments WHERE video_id=? ORDER BY start_time",
        (vid,)).fetchall()
    con.close()
    return [(float(s), float(e)) for s, e in rows] if rows else None


def _gemini_rallies(stem: str):
    f = os.path.join("output", f"gemini_{stem}_19june.json")
    if not os.path.exists(f):
        return None
    return [(float(s), float(e)) for s, e in json.load(open(f))]


def _dur_stats(rs):
    d = sorted(e - s for s, e in rs)
    return (statistics.median(d) if d else 0.0, max(d) if d else 0.0)


def main() -> None:
    print(f"{'clip':10} | {'L_tot':>5} {'G_tot':>5} | {'L>7':>4} {'G>7':>4} | "
          f"{'match':>5} {'L-only':>6} {'G-only':>6} | {'L_med':>5} {'G_med':>5} {'L_max':>5} {'G_max':>5}")
    print("-" * 100)
    agg = {"L": 0, "G": 0, "L7": 0, "G7": 0, "match": 0, "Lonly": 0, "Gonly": 0}
    for stem in STEMS:
        local = _local_rallies(stem)
        gem = _gemini_rallies(stem)
        if local is None or gem is None:
            print(f"{stem:10} | local={'?' if local is None else len(local)} "
                  f"gemini={'?' if gem is None else len(gem)}  (incomplete — skipping)")
            continue
        l7 = sum(1 for s, e in local if e - s > LONG)
        g7 = sum(1 for s, e in gem if e - s > LONG)
        # IoU agreement (0.3 — lenient, just "same rally"): match local<->gemini
        mr = metrics.match_intervals(local, gem, iou_threshold=0.3)
        match = len(mr.matches)
        l_only = len(local) - match           # local fires, Gemini doesn't (local over-fire / FP-ish)
        g_only = len(gem) - match             # Gemini fires, local doesn't (local miss)
        lmed, lmax = _dur_stats(local)
        gmed, gmax = _dur_stats(gem)
        print(f"{stem:10} | {len(local):5} {len(gem):5} | {l7:4} {g7:4} | "
              f"{match:5} {l_only:6} {g_only:6} | {lmed:5.1f} {gmed:5.1f} {lmax:5.1f} {gmax:5.1f}")
        agg["L"] += len(local); agg["G"] += len(gem); agg["L7"] += l7; agg["G7"] += g7
        agg["match"] += match; agg["Lonly"] += l_only; agg["Gonly"] += g_only
    print("-" * 100)
    print(f"{'TOTAL':10} | {agg['L']:5} {agg['G']:5} | {agg['L7']:4} {agg['G7']:4} | "
          f"{agg['match']:5} {agg['Lonly']:6} {agg['Gonly']:6}")
    if agg["G"]:
        print(f"\nLocal/Gemini total ratio: {agg['L']/agg['G']:.2f}x  "
              f"(>1 = local over-fires)")
    if agg["match"]:
        print(f"Local precision vs Gemini (match/L_tot): {agg['match']/agg['L']:.2f}  "
              f"Local recall vs Gemini (match/G_tot): {agg['match']/agg['G']:.2f}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

### 343. assistant (2026-06-19T08:55:31.188Z)

Clear gap: when a chunk's `generate` exhausts retries (timeout or 503), the provider logs an error but returns `[], metrics` with **no failure flag** — so the segmenter can't distinguish "chunk failed" from "chunk had no rallies," and silently ships a partial result. That's exactly what bit GX010142. Clean fix: flag the failure in metrics + have the segmenter surface it loudly. Let me read the segmenter's chunk aggregation:

# Boxhill 19-June — local extraction, Gemini comparison & gap learnings

## 📦 Deliverables (on G:)
`G:\My Drive\Sharing\Badminton\Boxhill\2026-06-19 Boxhill - Local no-AI (7s+ rallies)\`
- **GX010142** — 44 rallies → **3 ≥7s** reel · **GX010143** — 38 → **7** · **GX010145** — 55 → **6** (all local, cap=6+R4 milestone config, `--min-rally-duration 7`).
- **GX010144 + GX010147** re-running now (they hit the 30-min WASB timeout; ~1hr to land 5 reels total).
- Gemini reference detections for 142/143/145 saved for your golden-labeling pass.

## 🔬 The learnings — local vs Gemini (your "identify the gaps" ask)
3 single-court clips, IoU-0.3 "same rally" matching (`scratch/analyze_19june.py`):

| | Local | Gemini | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| total rallies | **137** | 66 | **2.08× over-fire** |
| recall-vs-Gemini | — | — | **0.85** (local finds 85% of Gemini's) |
| precision-vs-Gemini | — | — | **0.41** (59% of local's are local-only) |
| **rallies >7s** | **16** | 13 | close on *count*… |
| **>7s that agree** | — | — | **only 5 match** (long-rally precision 0.31 / recall 0.38) |

**Three gaps, all confirmed on fresh footage (they map to your gap-closure doc):**
1. **G2 — over-fire (~2×), and it's mostly SHORT.** Local fires 137 vs Gemini's 66. Restricting to >7s nearly closes the *count* gap (16 vs 13) — i.e. the bulk of local's extra rallies are short. This is exactly your P7 long-rally thesis holding up.
2. **G3 — long-rally over-merge / over-extension (the real surprise).** Among >7s rallies the counts are close but **agreement is poor — only 5 of local's 16 long windows match a Gemini long rally.** Local's long windows tend to be *merges/over-extensions* that don't map 1:1 to Gemini's cleaner segmentation — e.g. GX010145 has a **43s** local "rally" (a degenerate blob). So local's >7s reels mix real long rallies **with merge artifacts**; the cap=6 windowing limits but doesn't eliminate them.
3. **Local's strength: recall.** It rarely misses what Gemini finds (0.85), and only 10 rallies were Gemini-only across all 3 clips.

**Important caveat (honest):** "local-only" ≠ "false positive" — some of those 81 are real rallies Gemini *missed* (Gemini under-detects too). Only your **golden labeling** of 142/143/145 resolves who's right — at which point these become real accuracy rows in the gap-closure doc (vs the current agreement-only read).

## 🛠️ Due-diligence — 4 PRs found, fixed, merged today
- [#237](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/237) **master-breaking**: a moved-symbol reference broke the *entire* local suite at collection (green in CI only because the golden data is absent there). · [#238](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/238) `--min-rally-duration` flag · [#239](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/239) nightly re-baseline to n=15 · [#240](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/240) Gemini fails loudly on dropped chunks instead of shipping silent partial reels.

## 🚩 For your decision (owner-gated, I did not touch)
- **Shipped model alias `gemini-flash-latest` is degraded right now** (11.6s trivial vs 2.5-flash's 1.3s) — this silently broke the Gemini path until I pinned 2.5-flash. Worth deciding whether to pin a specific model.
- **WASB 30-min timeout is too tight for 12-min+ clips** (they legitimately need ~43min at ~3.4× realtime) — consider a length-aware default, or raise `indexing.wasb.timeout_sec`.

I'll confirm once the GX010144/147 reels finish. Verified the whole spine locally (suite 904 green, ruff+mypy clean, eval unchanged); GPU/WSL ran on your machine end-to-end.

### 406. assistant (2026-06-19T13:06:53.601Z)

"""Full local-vs-Gemini head-to-head report for the Boxhill 19-June clips (owner request).

Gemini = REFERENCE (not ground truth — D3); this measures how local & Gemini DIFFER, not who is
correct (that needs golden labels). Emits a markdown report to output/19june_local_vs_gemini.md.
Local rallies = output/19june.db (shipped cap=6+R4 milestone windowing); Gemini = the
output/gemini_<stem>_19june.json reference detections. CPU-only; gitignored scratch."""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import sqlite3
import statistics
import sys

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))

from backend.eval import metrics
from backend.utils.hashing import compute_video_id

PROXY_DIR = r"F:/KhelSutra_work/19june_proxies"
STEMS = ["GX010142", "GX010143", "GX010144", "GX010145", "GX010147"]
LOCAL_DB = os.path.join("output", "19june.db")
OUT_MD = os.path.join("output", "19june_local_vs_gemini.md")
LONG = 7.0


def _local(stem):
    proxy = os.path.join(PROXY_DIR, f"{stem}_1080p.mp4")
    if not os.path.exists(proxy):
        return None
    vid = compute_video_id(proxy)
    con = sqlite3.connect(LOCAL_DB)
    rows = con.execute("SELECT start_time,end_time FROM play_segments WHERE video_id=? ORDER BY start_time",
                       (vid,)).fetchall()
    con.close()
    return [(float(s), float(e)) for s, e in rows] if rows else None


def _gem(stem):
    f = os.path.join("output", f"gemini_{stem}_19june.json")
    return [(float(s), float(e)) for s, e in json.load(open(f))] if os.path.exists(f) else None


def _stats(rs):
    d = sorted(e - s for s, e in rs)
    if not d:
        return (0.0, 0.0, 0.0)
    return (statistics.median(d), d[int(len(d) * 0.75)] if len(d) > 1 else d[0], max(d))


def _match(a, b, iou):
    return len(metrics.match_intervals(a, b, iou_threshold=iou).matches)


def main() -> None:
    L = ["# Boxhill 19-June — local vs Gemini head-to-head", "",
         "**Reference, not ground truth.** Gemini is the *reference* (per gap-closure D3), not truth — "
         "this measures how the local detector and Gemini **differ**, not which is more accurate. "
         "True accuracy needs the golden labels (the labeling pass). Local = shipped cap=6 + R4 "
         "milestone windowing (`output/19june.db`); Gemini = `gemini-2.5-flash` reference detections.",
         "", "## All rallies (IoU 0.3 = 'found the same rally')", "",
         "| clip | local | gemini | over-fire | match@.3 | match@.5 | local-only | gemini-only | "
         "L med/p75/max | G med/p75/max |", "|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|"]
    agg = {k: 0 for k in ("L", "G", "L7", "G7", "m3", "m5", "Lo", "Go", "m7")}
    clips = 0
    longrows = []
    for stem in STEMS:
        loc, gem = _local(stem), _gem(stem)
        if loc is None or gem is None:
            L.append(f"| {stem} | {'—' if loc is None else len(loc)} | "
                     f"{'—' if gem is None else len(gem)} | (incomplete) | | | | | | |")
            continue
        clips += 1
        m3, m5 = _match(loc, gem, 0.3), _match(loc, gem, 0.5)
        lo, go = len(loc) - m3, len(gem) - m3
        lmed, lp75, lmax = _stats(loc)
        gmed, gp75, gmax = _stats(gem)
        ratio = len(loc) / len(gem) if gem else 0
        L.append(f"| {stem} | {len(loc)} | {len(gem)} | {ratio:.2f}x | {m3} | {m5} | {lo} | {go} | "
                 f"{lmed:.1f}/{lp75:.1f}/{lmax:.1f} | {gmed:.1f}/{gp75:.1f}/{gmax:.1f} |")
        # long-rally regime
        lL = [(s, e) for s, e in loc if e - s > LONG]
        gL = [(s, e) for s, e in gem if e - s > LONG]
        m7 = _match(lL, gL, 0.3)
        longrows.append((stem, len(lL), len(gL), m7))
        for k, v in (("L", len(loc)), ("G", len(gem)), ("L7", len(lL)), ("G7", len(gL)),
                     ("m3", m3), ("m5", m5), ("Lo", lo), ("Go", go), ("m7", m7)):
            agg[k] += v
    if clips:
        L += ["", f"**Aggregate (n={clips}):** local **{agg['L']}** vs Gemini **{agg['G']}** rallies = "
              f"**{agg['L']/agg['G']:.2f}× over-fire**. "
              f"recall-vs-Gemini **{agg['m3']/agg['G']:.2f}** (local finds this fraction of Gemini's), "
              f"precision-vs-Gemini **{agg['m3']/agg['L']:.2f}** (this fraction of local's match a Gemini "
              f"rally; the rest are local-only — FP **or** Gemini-miss, golden resolves). "
              f"Boundary-strict agreement drops {agg['m3']}→{agg['m5']} at IoU 0.5."]
    # long-rally table
    L += ["", "## Long rallies (>7s) — the product regime", "",
          "| clip | local>7 | gemini>7 | match>7 |", "|---|---|---|---|"]
    for stem, l7, g7, m7 in longrows:
        L.append(f"| {stem} | {l7} | {g7} | {m7} |")
    if longrows:
        L.append(f"| **TOTAL** | **{agg['L7']}** | **{agg['G7']}** | **{agg['m7']}** |")
        if agg["L7"] and agg["G7"]:
            L += ["", f"**>7s read:** counts are close (local {agg['L7']} / Gemini {agg['G7']}) but "
                  f"agreement is LOW (only {agg['m7']} match) → local's long windows largely don't "
                  f"correspond 1:1 to Gemini's long rallies. Local **over-merges / over-extends** "
                  f"(watch the local max-duration column above) — a >7s local window often spans what "
                  f"Gemini sees as multiple shorter rallies, or runs past the rally end."]
    L += ["", "## What this says about the gaps", "",
          "- **G2 — over-fire (mostly short):** local fires ~2× Gemini overall; restricting to >7s nearly "
          "closes the *count* gap, so the bulk of the excess is short rallies (motion blips / "
          "background-court fragments). Filtering to long rallies strips most of it.",
          "- **G3 — long-rally over-merge / over-extension:** where local *does* fire long, its windows "
          "disagree with Gemini's segmentation (low >7s match, IoU-0.3→0.5 drop) — merges + rally-end "
          "over-run. The cap=6 windowing limits but doesn't remove these.",
          "- **Local's strength — recall:** it rarely misses what Gemini finds (high recall-vs-Gemini); "
          "few Gemini-only rallies.",
          "", "## Caveats",
          "- **Agreement, not accuracy.** 'local-only' is NOT 'false positive' — Gemini under-detects "
          "too. Only golden labels rank correctness. Label 142/143/145(+144/147) → real accuracy.",
          "- Gemini chunks that hit capacity 503s after retries drop their time range (now surfaced "
          "loudly per #240); affected clips may slightly under-count Gemini rallies.",
          "- IoU-0.3 is a lenient 'same rally' threshold; IoU-0.5 adds boundary agreement."]
    out = "\n".join(L)
    with open(OUT_MD, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        fh.write(out)
    print(out)
    print(f"\n[written {OUT_MD}]")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

# Boxhill 19-June — local vs Gemini head-to-head

**Reference, not ground truth.** Gemini is the *reference* (per gap-closure D3), not truth — this measures how the local detector and Gemini **differ**, not which is more accurate. True accuracy needs the golden labels (the labeling pass). Local = shipped cap=6 + R4 milestone windowing (`output/19june.db`); Gemini = `gemini-2.5-flash` reference detections.

## All rallies (IoU 0.3 = 'found the same rally')

| clip | local | gemini | over-fire | match@.3 | match@.5 | local-only | gemini-only | L med/p75/max | G med/p75/max |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GX010142 | 44 | 24 | 1.83x | 20 | 17 | 24 | 4 | 4.3/5.5/8.1 | 3.9/4.8/11.0 |
| GX010143 | 38 | 20 | 1.90x | 19 | 12 | 19 | 1 | 5.5/6.3/9.4 | 4.4/8.0/13.5 |
| GX010144 | 58 | — | (incomplete) | | | | | | |
| GX010145 | 55 | 22 | 2.50x | 17 | 15 | 38 | 5 | 4.3/5.7/12.4 | 4.1/7.0/43.4 |
| GX010147 | 40 | — | (incomplete) | | | | | | |

**Aggregate (n=3):** local **137** vs Gemini **66** rallies = **2.08× over-fire**. recall-vs-Gemini **0.85** (local finds this fraction of Gemini's), precision-vs-Gemini **0.41** (this fraction of local's match a Gemini rally; the rest are local-only — FP **or** Gemini-miss, golden resolves). Boundary-strict agreement drops 56→44 at IoU 0.5.

## Long rallies (>7s) — the product regime

| clip | local>7 | gemini>7 | match>7 |
|---|---|---|---|
| GX010142 | 3 | 2 | 1 |
| GX010143 | 7 | 6 | 2 |
| GX010145 | 6 | 5 | 2 |
| **TOTAL** | **16** | **13** | **5** |

**>7s read:** counts are close (local 16 / Gemini 13) but agreement is LOW (only 5 match) → local's long windows largely don't correspond 1:1 to Gemini's long rallies. Local **over-merges / over-extends** (watch the local max-duration column above) — a >7s local window often spans what Gemini sees as multiple shorter rallies, or runs past the rally end.

## What this says about the gaps

- **G2 — over-fire (mostly short):** local fires ~2× Gemini overall; restricting to >7s nearly closes the *count* gap, so the bulk of the excess is short rallies (motion blips / background-court fragments). Filtering to long rallies strips most of it.
- **G3 — long-rally over-merge / over-extension:** where local *does* fire long, its windows disagree with Gemini's segmentation (low >7s match, IoU-0.3→0.5 drop) — merges + rally-end over-run. The cap=6 windowing limits but doesn't remove these.
- **Local's strength — recall:** it rarely misses what Gemini finds (high recall-vs-Gemini); few Gemini-only rallies.

## Caveats
- **Agreement, not accuracy.** 'local-only' is NOT 'false positive' — Gemini under-detects too. Only golden labels rank correctness. Label 142/143/145(+144/147) → real accuracy.
- Gemini chunks that hit capacity 503s after retries drop their time range (now surfaced loudly per #240); affected clips may slightly under-count Gemini rallies.
- IoU-0.3 is a lenient 'same rally' threshold; IoU-0.5 adds boundary agreement.

## Boxhill 19-June — local vs Gemini (consolidated)

> **Reference, not ground truth.** Gemini is the *reference*, not truth — this is how local & Gemini **differ**, not who's more accurate. Real quality needs your golden labels.

**All rallies** (IoU 0.3 = "same rally"; IoU 0.5 adds boundary agreement):

| clip | local | gemini | over-fire | match@.3 | match@.5 | local-only | gemini-only |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GX010142 | 44 | 24 | 1.83× | 20 | 17 | 24 | 4 |
| GX010143 | 38 | 20 | 1.90× | 19 | 12 | 19 | 1 |
| GX010145 | 55 | 22 | 2.50× | 17 | 15 | 38 | 5 |

**Aggregate (n=3):** local **137** vs Gemini **66** = **2.08× over-fire** · recall-vs-Gemini **0.85** · precision-vs-Gemini **0.41** · boundary-strict match 56→**44** at IoU 0.5.

**Long rallies (>7s)** — the product regime:

| clip | local>7 | gemini>7 | match>7 |
|---|---|---|---|
| GX010142 | 3 | 2 | 1 |
| GX010143 | 7 | 6 | 2 |
| GX010145 | 6 | 5 | 2 |
| **TOTAL** | **16** | **13** | **5** |

**The three gaps:** **G2** local over-fires ~2× but it's mostly *short* rallies (the >7s count gap nearly closes). **G3** where local *does* fire long, it disagrees with Gemini (only 5 of 16 match; the 0.3→0.5 IoU drop confirms boundary over-extension) — local over-merges/over-runs (a 43s blob on 145). **Strength:** local rarely misses what Gemini finds (recall 0.85).

### 431. assistant (2026-06-19T15:40:35.577Z)

Yes — partly. The signal **is built as code**, but it's not validated or wired to do anything in production. Here's the precise state.

## The signal: `shuttle_player_colocation` (the held-shuttle / shuttle-in-hand discriminator)

**✅ Built — the feature math** ([fusion_features.py:168](backend/pipeline/detectors/fusion_features.py)): `shuttle_player_colocation(...)` computes the *fraction of shuttle-visible frames where the shuttle point sits inside/adjacent (a `pad_frac=0.03` halo) to a person's bounding box*. **Held/in-hand ⇒ high; in-flight ⇒ ~0** — exactly the discriminator. It's pure, GPU-free, and unit-tested (`test_fusion_features.py`, "colocation halo + alignment").

**✅ Wired into the FusionSegmenter** ([fusion_hybrid.py:88](backend/pipeline/segmenters/fusion_hybrid.py)): when the person cue is enabled (`indexing.fusion.cue_flags.person`), it lazily builds a `PersonDetector`, runs it over each window, and computes colocation as one of the `PERSON_FEATURE_NAMES`. Inputs (person boxes + WASB shuttle trajectory) both exist.

## But four big caveats — it doesn't actually *do* anything yet
1. **Default-OFF.** The person cue defaults off, so colocation isn't computed in the served/default path (keeps torch/GPU out of the hot path).
2. **It's a persisted *feature column*, not a *gate*.** Even with the person cue on, colocation is stored for a *learned fusion scorer* (the future P3 feature-fusion) — it doesn't currently filter or end any rally. Gate B uses player *occupancy* (`min_players`), not colocation.
3. **Mock-tested only — never validated on real footage** (the code says so verbatim: *"real-video verification is owner-side; this wiring is mock-tested"*). Person features have **never been extracted on the golden corpus** (gap-closure §6), so colocation's actual discriminative power / ΔF1 is **unmeasured**.
4. The learned scorer that would *weight* it isn't trained/promoted.

So the docs' `[design]` tag is **stale for the code** (the feature exists) but **accurate for production use** (off, ungated, unmeasured).

## Two relevant notes
- There's a **second, no-new-model approach** in the design that is *not* built: sustained **WASB `Visibility=0` after the last fast stroke** (a held shuttle "goes dark" — WASB can't track it), which wouldn't need person detection at all. I don't see code for it; it's a design idea in the gap-closure doc.
- **This is directly the fix for the G3 gap I measured today** — local's >7s windows over-extend/over-merge because, when a player picks up and holds the shuttle between points, shuttle-velocity alone can't tell "rally" from "reset." Colocation (or the visibility-dark proxy) is exactly that missing player-state signal.

Want me to take the obvious next step — **extract person features (incl. colocation) on a couple of the golden/19-June clips and actually measure whether it separates held-shuttle from in-flight**? That'd turn it from built-but-unproven into a real, ablated number.

### 451. assistant (2026-06-19T15:52:33.757Z)

# Prominent-Court Detection — kill background-court false positives (multicourt)

> **Status:** `DRAFT` — design + experiment **owner-gated for any default flip** · **Owner:** Avi · **Created:** 2026-06-19 · **Last updated:** 2026-06-19
> **Lifecycle:** `DRAFT → IN PROGRESS → DONE → archived` (move to `docs/archives/` when DONE)
> **Tracking anchors:** §8 progress tracker is the source of truth; pointer in memory `current-state`.
> **Relation to existing docs:** attacks gap **G2** ("precision — local over-fires, worst on multicourt") from [`RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md`](RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md); it is the **shuttle-side** counterpart of the person-side **Gate B / court mask** in [`MULTI_SIGNAL_FUSION_PLAN.md`](MULTI_SIGNAL_FUSION_PLAN.md). Complements the held-shuttle **`shuttle_player_colocation`** (G3) — court-localization (G2) and held-shuttle (G3) are independent precision levers.
> **Honesty note:** claims are tagged `[verified]` (checked vs code/measured) / `[design]` (proposed). Every signal lands **flag-gated, default-OFF**, promoted only on measured lift per [`EXPERIMENT_HARNESS.md`](EXPERIMENT_HARNESS.md) + [[decision-rigor-norm]].

---

## 0. TL;DR
In **multicourt** footage the camera sees several courts. The shuttle detector (WASB) tracks *any* shuttle in frame, so a rally on a **background / adjacent court** becomes a predicted rally on the **main match** — a false positive. The fix: identify the **prominent court** (the foreground court that occupies the majority of the frame, where the match being filmed is played) and **keep only rallies whose shuttle lives inside it**. Mechanically this re-defines the shuttle signal from `(x, y, visible)` to `(x, y, visible-AND-inside-the-prominent-court)` *before* windowing — so a background-court shuttle reads as "not there" and never forms a rally. A flat 2D floor polygon is the MVP; a **pseudo-3D play-volume** (court floor + the air column above it) is the refinement so a high clear over the prominent court isn't wrongly rejected. Lands as a **default-OFF experiment**; the flip is gated on a Launch Report ([[launch-report-convention]]).

## 1. Problem & motivating example `[verified]`
- **Measured gap (G2):** local over-fires ~2× vs Gemini, and the over-fire is **worst on multicourt** — even among long (>7s) rallies, multicourt over-fires **1.58×** (background-court games are *real* long rallies, just on the wrong court). See `RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md` §3 + the P7 long-rally lens.
- **Concrete anchor — GX010142 rally #1 (2026-06-19).** Owner observation: *"the first rally is just a shuttle flying in the non-prominent court… while the prominent-court players have not started."* Confirmed from the cached WASB trajectory (`output/wasb/GX010142_1080p__*_wasb.csv`): rally #1 (`0.72–3.65s`, 2.94s) has a shuttle centroid at **meanX ≈ 1616** on a 1920-wide frame (≈84% to the right) — spatially apart from the central cluster where most rallies live (meanX ≈ 1000–1300). Several other suspicious rallies (#3, #9, #12, #16) cluster in the same far-right band → consistent with a **right-side adjacent court**.
- **Why a 1D threshold is not enough `[verified]`:** the courts do not separate cleanly on X or Y alone (rally Y-centroids overlap 250–620 px across courts; the far-right band mixes with foreground play). The courts are **2D regions** under perspective — so we need the court *geometry*, not a coordinate cutoff. This is the owner's "pseudo-3D / court model" intuition, validated by the data.

## 2. Goal & non-goals
- **Goal:** on multicourt clips, drop rallies whose shuttle is outside the prominent court, **without losing real prominent-court rallies** — measured per-video, no regression on single-court clips (where it must be a no-op or a do-no-harm).
- **Non-goals (v1):** multi-camera; tracking players across courts; full court-line homography for scoring; anything biometric. Single-court footage is explicitly a **no-op** (one court ⇒ everything is "prominent").

## 3. Decisions locked
| # | Decision | Rationale |
|---|----------|-----------|
| D1 | The prominent court is a **2D region (polygon) in image space**, refined to a **pseudo-3D play volume** | 1D thresholds don't separate courts under perspective (§1) |
| D2 | Gate the **shuttle trajectory** by the region **before windowing** (re-define visibility) | Stops a background rally at the source — no window ever forms there. Reuses `point_in_polygon` |
| D3 | **Default-OFF, flag-gated; single-court = no-op** | Zero-regression rollout ([[weak-signals-retained]]); no behaviour change unless a polygon is supplied + the flag set |
| D4 | Reuse the existing **`court_polygon`** config + `point_in_polygon`; do NOT invent a new geometry stack | `FusionConfig.court_polygon` + `fusion_features.point_in_polygon` already exist `[verified]` |
| D5 | The prominent court can be **supplied (config) OR auto-derived**; auto-derivation is its own measured step | Config polygon is the cheapest first experiment; auto is the product path |

## 4. Design

### 4.1 The signal change (the core experiment)
Today the windower consumes the raw WASB trajectory: a per-frame `(Frame, Visibility, X, Y)`. The change is a **pre-windowing filter**:

```
for each frame f:  if shuttle_visible(f) and NOT inside_prominent_court(X_f, Y_f):  mark f as NOT visible
```

A background-court shuttle is then indistinguishable from "no shuttle" → the velocity windower never opens a rally there. This is purely **subtractive** on the shuttle stream (it can only remove visibility, never add it), so on a correctly-drawn prominent court it cannot manufacture rallies — only suppress out-of-court ones. (Mirror of `rally_gate`'s "do-no-harm" discipline.)

### 4.2 Identifying the prominent court (four routes, cheapest first)
1. **Configured polygon** (MVP, `[design]→buildable now`): a normalized `[[x,y],…]` court polygon per camera/clip, exactly the existing `FusionConfig.court_polygon`. Draw it once from the dense shuttle/player cluster. Good enough to validate the whole concept on GX010142 + the multicourt golden clips.
2. **Player-location auto-derivation** (`[design]`): run `PersonDetector.detections_per_frame` `[verified] exists`, take the **largest, most-central, most-persistent** player cluster (the foreground players are bigger boxes lower in frame), and fit the prominent-court polygon to where *those* players' feet live. "Prominent = where the match's players are." This is why the owner asked to **extract person features** — players anchor the court.
3. **Shuttle-density auto-derivation** (`[design]`): the prominent court is the densest sustained shuttle-activity region; cluster the visible trajectory and take the dominant mode. Person-free fallback.
4. **Court-line detection** (`[design]`, heaviest): Hough/segmentation court lines → the largest court quad. Most accurate, most work; deferred.

v1 ships route 1 (config) + measures; route 2 (player-anchored) is the product upgrade.

### 4.3 Pseudo-3D play volume (the refinement)
A flat floor polygon rejects a shuttle at the top of a high clear *over the prominent court* (it projects above the floor quad). The pseudo-3D model treats the prominent court as a **floor quad + a vertical air column**: accept a shuttle if its image point lies within the floor polygon **expanded upward** by a height envelope (the max plausible shuttle height projected through the camera). Practically: the floor quad plus an upward `pad` (perspective-aware — larger near the net/far baseline). Start with a generous fixed upward pad (cheap, MVP); upgrade to a per-camera height envelope only if measurement shows real rallies being clipped. This keeps the floor tight (rejects adjacent-court floor play) while not clipping legitimate high shuttles.

### 4.4 Reuse map `[verified]`
| Need | Existing code |
|---|---|
| point-in-region test | `backend/pipeline/detectors/fusion_features.py::point_in_polygon` |
| normalized court polygon config | `backend/config/models.py::FusionConfig.court_polygon` (today: person/Gate-B only) |
| player boxes per frame | `backend/pipeline/detectors/person_detector.py::PersonDetector.detections_per_frame` |
| trajectory parse + windowing | `backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.parse_trajectory_csv`, `backend/eval/windowing.py` |
| per-video scoring | `backend/eval/rally_seg_eval.py` (+ the `--stratify` multicourt split, #220) |

New code is small: a `prominent_court_gate(points, polygon, height_pad)` that filters trajectory points, threaded into the windowing input behind a default-OFF flag.

## 5. Experiment plan (measurement)
Land the gate flag-gated, then measure on the **cached trajectories** (CPU, no GPU/re-detect):
1. **GX010142 sanity:** draw a prominent-court polygon; confirm **rally #1 disappears** and the central rallies survive. The litmus the whole idea is born from.
2. **Multicourt golden** (`mahadevpura_2, GX010128, Badminton_BXH_2, Boxhill_Doubles`): Δ rally-count, and once those clips are golden-labelled, Δ precision / Δ recall vs golden via `rally_seg_eval --stratify` (single vs multicourt). The win condition: multicourt precision up, **recall flat** (we drop FPs, not real rallies).
3. **Single-court no-op:** on single-court clips with no polygon (or a full-frame polygon) the gate is a verified no-op — assert zero change.
4. **Dual-eval discipline** ([[eval-dual-set-regression]]): no per-video regression on either eval set.

Promotion to default-ON is a **separate, owner-gated** decision with a Launch Report (both rulers + the over-seg guard), never bundled with the experiment PR.

## 6. Risks & limits
- **Polygon is camera-dependent.** A hand-drawn polygon doesn't generalize across setups → route 2/3 (auto-derivation) is the real product path; v1 config-polygon is for *validation*.
- **Shuttle height** (§4.3): too-tight a floor clips high clears; the pseudo-3D pad mitigates, measurement tunes it.
- **The prominent court can change** (camera re-aim mid-clip): out of scope v1 (assume static per clip).
- **Not a recall fix.** This only removes out-of-court FPs; it does nothing for missed in-court rallies (G1) — and must be proven not to *cost* recall.

## 7. Open questions (owner / data)
1. Auto-derivation anchor: **player-location** (route 2) vs **shuttle-density** (route 3) as the default once validated? *(decides: data, post-experiment)*
2. Height-envelope: fixed upward pad vs per-camera perspective model — is the fixed pad enough? *(decides: measurement)*
3. Do we expose the prominent court as a **one-time per-camera setup** (draw-once UI) or always auto? *(decides: owner/product)*

## 8. Deliverables & progress tracker ⟵ source of truth
Legend: ☐ Todo · ◐ In progress · ☑ Done · ⛔ Gated. One small PR per row, off `origin/master`, no stacking.

| ID | Deliverable | Depends on | Gated? | Status | PR |
|----|-------------|-----------|--------|--------|----|
| C0 | This design doc | — | No | ☑ | this PR |
| C1 | `prominent_court_gate(points, polygon, height_pad)` + unit tests (pure, point-in-polygon) | — | No (default-OFF) | ☐ | — |
| C2 | Thread the gate into the windowing input behind a default-OFF flag (`court_polygon` + `gate_shuttle_to_court`) | C1 | No (default-OFF) | ☐ | — |

## 9. Definition of done
- [ ] The gate exists, is **default-OFF**, unit-tested, and a verified no-op on single-court / no-polygon.
- [ ] GX010142 rally #1 (and the far-right adjacent-court band) is dropped while the central rallies survive `[measured]`.
- [ ] On the multicourt golden clips, multicourt **precision improves with recall flat** vs golden labels (no per-video regression).
- [ ] A winning configuration is flipped default-ON only via a Launch Report → flip `Status → DONE`, archive.

The spine: **no special-casing in the decision path** (signals→scorer, never `if/else`). Worked examples: a service-fault analyzer, a warm-up/practice span detector, and consuming a per-frame shuttle-state detector. def point_in_polygon(point: Tuple[float, float], polygon: Sequence[Tuple[float, float]]) -> bool:
    """Ray-casting point-in-polygon. `point` and `polygon` share a coordinate space
    (we use normalised 0-1). Empty/degenerate polygon (<3 vertices) ⇒ False."""
    if polygon is None or len(polygon) < 3:
        return False
    x, y = point
    inside = False
    n = len(polygon)
    j = n - 1
    for i in range(n):
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        # Does the horizontal ray at y cross edge (i, j)?
        if (yi > y) != (yj > y):
            x_cross = (xj - xi) * (y - yi) / (yj - yi) + xi if yj != yi else xi
            if x < x_cross:

### 466. assistant (2026-06-19T15:56:27.488Z)

"""Prominent-court shuttle gate — multicourt G2 (see ``docs/PROMINENT_COURT_DETECTION.md``).

In multicourt footage the WASB shuttle tracker follows ANY shuttle in frame, so a rally on a
background / adjacent court becomes a false rally on the filmed match. This gate re-defines the
shuttle signal ``(x, y, visible)`` → ``(x, y, visible AND inside-the-prominent-court)``: a visible
shuttle point whose normalised position is outside the prominent-court polygon is marked NOT
visible, so the velocity windower never opens a rally there.

Pure (no GPU / cv2): point-in-polygon over the court polygon (reuses
``fusion_features.point_in_polygon``). **Subtractive only** — it can only CLEAR visibility, never
set it — so on a correct polygon it suppresses out-of-court rallies without manufacturing any.
``polygon=None`` (or < 3 vertices, or non-positive frame dims) ⇒ an EXACT no-op (single-court /
unconfigured footage), matching ``rally_gate``'s do-no-harm discipline.

The polygon is the court FLOOR in normalised 0-1 image coords (same convention as
``FusionConfig.court_polygon``). ``height_pad`` is the MVP pseudo-3D play-volume (§4.3): a point up
to ``height_pad`` (normalised) ABOVE the floor — i.e. higher in frame, smaller y — is still
admitted, so a high clear over the prominent court is not clipped.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import replace
from typing import Any, List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple

from backend.pipeline.detectors.fusion_features import point_in_polygon

Polygon = Sequence[Tuple[float, float]]


def admitted(nx: float, ny: float, polygon: Polygon, height_pad: float = 0.0) -> bool:
    """Is the normalised point inside the court floor polygon, OR within ``height_pad`` above it?

    The pseudo-3D air column: shifting the point DOWN by ``height_pad`` (toward the floor) and
    finding it inside means the original sits within ``height_pad`` above the floor → a high shuttle
    over the prominent court, admit it."""
    if point_in_polygon((nx, ny), polygon):
        return True
    return height_pad > 0.0 and point_in_polygon((nx, ny + height_pad), polygon)


def gate_points_to_court(points: List[Any], polygon: Optional[Polygon],
                         frame_width: float, frame_height: float,
                         height_pad: float = 0.0) -> List[Any]:
    """Return ``points`` with visibility CLEARED for any visible shuttle outside the prominent-court
    polygon (expanded upward by ``height_pad``).

    No-op (returns the input list unchanged) when ``polygon`` is None/degenerate or the frame dims
    are non-positive. Otherwise each gated point is a NEW ``TrajectoryPoint`` (the input list and
    its points are never mutated)."""
    if not polygon or len(polygon) < 3 or frame_width <= 0 or frame_height <= 0:
        return points
    out: List[Any] = []
    for p in points:
        if getattr(p, "visible", False):
            nx, ny = p.x / frame_width, p.y / frame_height
            if not admitted(nx, ny, polygon, height_pad):
                p = replace(p, visible=False)
        out.append(p)
    return out

-We are using the **growing golden corpus** (human rally labels) to find and close the rally-detection accuracy gap **per video, before launch** — and the first ground-truth clip already reshaped the picture. The boundary work (R2/R3/R4) **holds** — when local fires on a real rally, its edges are tight (|Δ| < 1 s). **The remaining gap is DETECTION, not boundaries:** (G1) **recall** — both local and Gemini miss ~half the rallies on hard clips; (G2) **precision** — local over-fires (false positives), especially on continuous drilling; (G3) **rally-END over-extension** — when a player *quickly knocks the shuttle back after a point*, shuttle-velocity alone can't tell "rally" from "casual reset," so the window runs long / merges. n=2 sharpens the priority: **LOCAL = high recall / low precision, GEMINI = high precision / low recall** (opposite, complementary — on the normal clip local's recall *beats* Gemini's). So **the #1 lever is precision** (cut local's false positives via `rally_gate` Gate-A + colocation; the player-state rally-END rule feeds this), with **signal fusion** (local's recall + Gemini-style precision) as the durable win. **Caveat:** two golden clips so far (n=13 corpus, GX010141+GX010137) — directional, not conclusive.

### 491. assistant (2026-06-19T16:03:05.711Z)

"""Unit tests for the prominent-court shuttle gate (backend.pipeline.detectors.court_gate).

Pure point-in-polygon math on synthetic TrajectoryPoints — no GPU/video. Locks the load-bearing
guarantees: subtractive-only, no-op when unconfigured, never mutates the input, and the pseudo-3D
height pad admits high shuttles over the court. See docs/PROMINENT_COURT_DETECTION.md.
"""
from backend.pipeline.detectors.court_gate import admitted, gate_points_to_court
from backend.pipeline.detectors.tracknet_runner import TrajectoryPoint

# Left-half court (normalised): x in [0, 0.5], full height.
LEFT_HALF = [(0.0, 0.0), (0.5, 0.0), (0.5, 1.0), (0.0, 1.0)]
# A horizontal mid band: y in [0.4, 0.6], full width (for the height-pad test).
MID_BAND = [(0.0, 0.4), (1.0, 0.4), (1.0, 0.6), (0.0, 0.6)]
W = H = 1000.0


def _pts():
    return [
        TrajectoryPoint(frame=0, visible=True, x=200.0, y=500.0),   # norm (0.2,0.5) — inside left half
        TrajectoryPoint(frame=1, visible=True, x=800.0, y=500.0),   # norm (0.8,0.5) — outside (right court)
        TrajectoryPoint(frame=2, visible=False, x=850.0, y=500.0),  # already invisible — untouched
    ]


def test_no_polygon_is_exact_noop():
    pts = _pts()
    assert gate_points_to_court(pts, None, W, H) is pts            # same list object, no work
    assert gate_points_to_court(pts, [], W, H) is pts
    assert gate_points_to_court(pts, [(0.0, 0.0), (1.0, 0.0)], W, H) is pts  # <3 verts


def test_nonpositive_frame_dims_noop():
    pts = _pts()
    assert gate_points_to_court(pts, LEFT_HALF, 0.0, H) is pts
    assert gate_points_to_court(pts, LEFT_HALF, W, 0.0) is pts


def test_gates_out_of_court_visible_points_only():
    out = gate_points_to_court(_pts(), LEFT_HALF, W, H)
    assert out[0].visible is True       # inside the prominent court — kept
    assert out[1].visible is False      # right-court shuttle — visibility cleared
    assert out[2].visible is False      # was already invisible — unchanged


def test_does_not_mutate_input():
    pts = _pts()
    gate_points_to_court(pts, LEFT_HALF, W, H)
    assert pts[1].visible is True        # the original point object is untouched (a new one is returned)


def test_subtractive_only_never_makes_visible():
    # An invisible point outside OR inside the court is never turned visible.
    pts = [TrajectoryPoint(frame=0, visible=False, x=200.0, y=500.0)]   # inside, but invisible
    out = gate_points_to_court(pts, LEFT_HALF, W, H)
    assert out[0].visible is False


def test_height_pad_admits_high_shuttle_over_court():
    # A point at y=0.35 sits just ABOVE the mid band [0.4,0.6] (a high clear over the court).
    high = [TrajectoryPoint(frame=0, visible=True, x=500.0, y=350.0)]   # norm (0.5, 0.35)
    # No pad → outside the floor band → gated out.
    assert gate_points_to_court(high, MID_BAND, W, H, height_pad=0.0)[0].visible is False
    # pad=0.1 → (0.5, 0.45) lands inside the band → admitted.
    assert gate_points_to_court(high, MID_BAND, W, H, height_pad=0.1)[0].visible is True


def test_admitted_helper():
    assert admitted(0.2, 0.5, LEFT_HALF) is True
    assert admitted(0.8, 0.5, LEFT_HALF) is False
    assert admitted(0.5, 0.35, MID_BAND, height_pad=0.0) is False
    assert admitted(0.5, 0.35, MID_BAND, height_pad=0.1) is True

261	
262	        Used by the fusion segmenter (MULTI_SIGNAL_FUSION_PLAN P2). Unlike
263	        ``extract_action_windows_from_chunk`` (which detects ITS OWN windows and discards the
264	        per-frame boxes), this returns the raw per-frame detections so the fusion features can
265	        align them with the shuttle trajectory. It reads the source by **absolute frame index**
266	        (seek + sequential read), NOT a re-extracted clip, so the returned frame indices match
267	        the WASB trajectory's ``.frame`` exactly — that alignment is what
268	        ``fusion_features.shuttle_player_colocation`` / ``two_sided_motion`` rely on.
269	
270	        Returns ``{"frames": {frame_idx: [(track_id, bbox), ...]}, "fps", "frame_width",
271	        "frame_height"}``. A fresh window-local ByteTrack tracker is used. ⚠ cv2 frame-seek
272	        accuracy varies by codec (it lands near the requested keyframe then decodes forward);
273	        treat the indices as ±a few frames on real footage — fine for window-level features.
274	        """
275	        self.lazy_load_model()
276	        cap = cv2.VideoCapture(video_path)
277	        if not cap.isOpened():
278	            logger.error(f"Could not open {video_path}")
279	            return {"frames": {}, "fps": 30.0, "frame_width": 0.0, "frame_height": 0.0}
280	
281	        fps = cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FPS)
282	        if fps <= 0:
283	            fps = 30.0
284	        frame_width = cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_WIDTH)
285	        frame_height = cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_HEIGHT)
286	        score_thresh = self.config.get("indexing", {}).get("detector_score_threshold", 0.5)
287	        step = max(frame_skip, 1)
288	        tracker = self.make_tracker(fps / step)
289	
290	        start_frame = max(0, int(start_frame))
291	        frames: Dict[int, List[Tuple[int, Tuple[float, float, float, float]]]] = {}
292	        cap.set(cv2.CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES, start_frame)
293	        frame_idx = start_frame
294	        while frame_idx <= end_frame:
295	            ret, frame = cap.read()
296	            if not ret:
297	                break
298	            if (frame_idx - start_frame) % step == 0:
299	                frames[frame_idx] = self.detect_and_track(frame, tracker, score_thresh)
300	            frame_idx += 1
301	        cap.release()
302	        return {"frames": frames, "fps": fps,

### 527. assistant (2026-06-19T16:12:07.353Z)

"""Extract person features on GX010142 (owner ask, prominent-court track C4).

Runs PersonDetector over the 1080p proxy → per-frame player boxes, aligned to the WASB trajectory
frames. Produces evidence for: (a) PLAYER-anchored prominent-court derivation (where do the
foreground players stand = the prominent court), (b) the rally-#1 "players have not started yet"
check (owner insight), and (c) shuttle_player_colocation (held vs in-flight). GPU; gitignored."""
from __future__ import annotations

import glob
import json
import os
import sys
from typing import Any, Dict, cast

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))

from backend.config import load_config
from backend.pipeline.detectors.person_detector import PersonDetector

PROXY = r"F:/KhelSutra_work/19june_proxies/GX010142_1080p.mp4"
OUT = os.path.join("output", "person_GX010142.json")
FRAME_SKIP = 10            # ~6 fps effective on a 59.94 fps proxy — plenty for occupancy/location
END_FRAME = 30000


def main() -> None:
    cfg = load_config()
    cfg.indexing.use_gpu = True
    det = PersonDetector(cast(Dict[str, Any], cfg))
    print(f"running PersonDetector on {PROXY} (frame_skip={FRAME_SKIP}) ...", flush=True)
    res = det.detections_per_frame(PROXY, 0, END_FRAME, frame_skip=FRAME_SKIP)
    frames = res["frames"]
    W, H = res["frame_width"], res["frame_height"]
    # Serialize compactly: {frame: [[cx_norm, foot_y_norm, area_norm], ...]} (foot point + box size).
    compact = {}
    nboxes = 0
    for fr, boxes in frames.items():
        rows = []
        for _tid, (x1, y1, x2, y2) in boxes:
            cx = (x1 + x2) / 2.0 / W
            fy = y2 / H                                   # foot = bottom of box
            area = ((x2 - x1) * (y2 - y1)) / (W * H)
            rows.append([round(cx, 4), round(fy, 4), round(area, 5)])
            nboxes += 1
        compact[int(fr)] = rows
    json.dump({"frames": compact, "fps": res["fps"], "frame_width": W, "frame_height": H},
              open(OUT, "w", encoding="utf-8"))
    counts = [len(v) for v in compact.values()]
    import statistics
    print(f"DONE: {len(frames)} sampled frames, {nboxes} person boxes -> {OUT}", flush=True)
    print(f"  players/frame: median {statistics.median(counts) if counts else 0}, "
          f"max {max(counts) if counts else 0}", flush=True)

## On your rally-end idea (captured → [#244](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/244))
I noted it as **§4.5 + tracker row C8** in the design doc: detect the **white court lines** → the **floor plane**; a shuttle **resting on the floor** (or landing **out-of-court**) **deterministically** ends the rally — the unambiguous shuttle-side rally-END signal for G3 (vs velocity heuristics that over-extend). One court-line detector feeds **both** the prominent-court polygon and the floor-contact end-rule. The one subtlety to measure: a true landing vs a shuttle dipped low then played back up (a dwell-at-floor check discriminates).

The person-feature extraction is still running on GPU — when it lands I'll auto-derive the prominent-court polygon from player location and re-run this list (it should drop the same adjacent-court rallies, but principled rather than a hand-drawn cut).

### 567. assistant (2026-06-19T16:28:52.926Z)

This investigation actually resolves the mystery — and the answer reframes it. Two findings:

1. **WASB outputs exactly one shuttle per frame** (the trajectory is a single `Frame,Visibility,X,Y` track — no multi-shuttle API in the current runner).
2. **Rally #3's tracked shuttle is 94% in the adjacent court** (peak x-norm **0.9**, 143 of 219 points; only 6% near the boundary at x≈0.7, no distinct prominent-court cluster).

So here's the honest reframe: **window 21-25 *is* the adjacent-court rally** — WASB locked onto the adjacent shuttle, so that window genuinely tracks the wrong court, and the gate removed it *correctly*. The legitimate prominent-court rally you saw at that moment was **never detected** — WASB was busy tracking the other shuttle. So it's not "the gate removed a legitimate rally"; it's "**single-shuttle detection missed the legitimate rally**, and separately tracked the adjacent one." **You can't save 21-25 by refining the gate — the prominent rally isn't in the data.** Saving it needs **multi-shuttle detection** (WASB emitting both shuttles, or running detection per court-region) — a detection-layer change, not a gate tweak. (Tuning the gate's `min_in_court_frac` down to keep 21-25 would also keep rally #1, your true FP — they're indistinguishable on a single-shuttle track: 6% vs 11% in-court.)

## My honest call: hand off to a clean slate
You're right that my context is filling, and this investigation just opened a meatier thread — **multi-shuttle detection** — that's a detection-layer design problem deserving fresh context and your design-mindset ramp-up. The two-shuttle mystery itself is *solved* (it's a detection limitation, not a gate bug), so this is a clean breakpoint. I'd rather hand you a sharp, well-prepared session than push this thread with a fuller context.

Let me check the running multicourt workflow so its result makes the handoff, then I'll write you a tight handoff:

## Multicourt analysis — done (`output/multicourt_analysis.md`)
The gate is a **real precision lever exactly where there's a separable adjacent court — and honestly null where there isn't:**

| clip | ΔP | ΔF1 | recall | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Boxhill_Doubles** | **+0.096** | **+0.078** | flat | **real win** — 20 adjacent-court FPs (all x>0.80) cut |
| mahadevpura_2 | +0.025 | +0.013 | flat | marginal — trims 4 edge FPs, no separable court |
| GX010128 | 0 | 0 | flat | null — over-fire is on-court over-merge |
| Badminton_BXH_2 | 0 | 0 | flat | null — FPs co-located with real rallies |

**Verdict:** multicourt over-fire has *three* causes — adjacent-court rallies (the gate fixes these), on-court over-merge (needs other levers), and **stuck-tracker artifacts** (a *second* detection-layer bug we found: WASB sometimes freezes on one exact pixel for minutes). The gate is the right tool for the first, not a cure-all. Recall stayed flat everywhere — no real rallies harmed.

---

## 🏸 Ice breaker for the next session

> **"Three shuttles, one match — which story is ours?"**
>
> Today we taught the system **where** the rally lives (the prominent court — it cleanly swept 20 background-court fires off Boxhill_Doubles without touching a single real rally), and we sketched **when** a rally ends (when the shuttle *kisses the floor* — your white-lines idea, deterministic and waiting to be built).
>
> But the clip that mattered most was the one we *couldn't* fix: rally 21-25, where two shuttles shared the frame and our detector was watching the wrong one. That's the whole next chapter in one frame — **the ceiling isn't the filter anymore, it's the eyes.** WASB hands us a single track; the rally we actually want might be on the other side of a net it never looked at.
>
> So the opening question to chew on: **if we could see every shuttle in the frame at once, how would we pick the one whose story is the match's?** Top-K tracking? One detector per court? A rally redefined as *"a shuttle, in the court that matters, until it hits the floor"*?
>
> Where → built. When → designed. **Which → the fun part, all yours.**
>
> *(P.S. — chase the ghost. Two clips had a shuttle frozen at the exact same pixel for minutes. A tracker that fell asleep mid-rally is a thread worth pulling. 👻)*

It's been a genuinely great session — 9 PRs, a real feature designed/built/validated, and an honest finding that points the way forward. The `▶ HANDOFF` block at the top of `current-state.md` will drop your next session straight into the **which-shuttle** puzzle. Go get 'em. 🏸
